[06:21:39.270] to me why like signals exist and why it's stuff like solid exists versus just using RxJS.
[06:21:45.300] I'm very familiar with Rx2JavaScript, having recently used it heavily in a big project, and aspects of the design and knockout are made with my Rx experience in mind. The key difference between knockout implementation and observer pattern in Rx is that knockout automatically infers the association and dependencies between observables from regular procedural code without you having to specify them up front through special functional API. I want knockout to use regular procedural imperative style code as it's more familiar and approachable for most developers. Another difference is Rx is optimized for composing streams and events without the state. At first I was enthusiastic about this and its functional purity, but after time it felt
[06:22:15.300] increasingly like I was jumping through awkward hoops and had to invent extra ways to simulate  state to manage UI commands efficiently. That's why in Knockout all the variables can be treated  as stateful. For example, you can always read their latest value, which is cache by the way,  it doesn't read compute until the underlying data changes. Rx goes further in Knockout into advanced  ways of composing event streams, whereas Knockout goes further into Rx into UI development, letting  you bind its observables to HTML DOM events and templates and manipulate them in any way you want.  Rx is great at what it does.
[06:22:42.030] which turned out not exactly what I wanted to build rich UIs, hence I designed Knockout.
